Fighting Friction 
to move

=== page 6 ===
FRICTION IS INHERENT TO MOTION. IN INDUSTRY, IT 
APPEARS AS INEFFICIENCIES, COMPLEXITY, DELAYS, AND UNTAPPED POTENTIAL. HOWEVER, FIGHTING FRICTION IS ESSENTIAL FOR PROGRESS AND THAT IS WHAT WE DO BEST.
The question has never been 
whether friction exists. It always does. The question is what one chooses to do about it.
At SKF India (Industrial), our answer 
has always been the same: Fighting Friction. Relentlessly, thoughtfully, and with the application expertise built through decades of partnership with the industries we serve.
In FY 2025-26, that commitment 
gained sharper focus. The demerger created a dedicated industrial organisation with the clarity and agility to serve sectors where reliability is critical and downtime comes at a high cost. In industries such as railways, renewable energy, metals, mining, and heavy engineering, that focus is not a luxury. It is what keeps industries moving forward.
During the year, we strengthened 
localisation, advanced digital solutions, aligned manufacturing with customer needs, and progressed our ‘Factory of the Future’ journey in Pune. Together, these initiatives help customers improve reliability, efficiency, and performance.
Yet fighting friction extends beyond technology and operations. It is reflected in the people we develop, the culture of ownership and safety we foster, and our commitment to sustainability and responsible growth.
Because progress is not defined by 
the absence of friction, but by the ability to overcome it.
That is what moves industries 
forward. And that is what drives SKF India (Industrial) every day.

=== page 9 ===
Adv Ancing 
through timeour Journey
Our journey reflects a steady evolution shaped by engineering excellence, expanding 
capabilities, and a deepening alignment with India’s industrial growth. From establishing an early presence in the country to building a strong manufacturing and technology footprint, each phase has strengthened the ability to serve diverse customer needs. This progression has culminated in a defining transformation in 2025, creating two focussed entities positioned to drive the next phase of growth with greater clarity, agility, and purpose.
1907 
Founded by Sven Wingquist 
following the invention of the self-aligning ball bearing, establishing a legacy in precision engineering1923 
Entry into India through trading operations in Calcutta (now Kolkata)1961 
Incorporated as Associated Bearing Company, strengthening presence in India.
2004
Established an Application Development Centre in Bengaluru to support localised engineering solutions
1964 
Commissioned the first manufacturing facility in Pune for rolling bearings
1989
Expanded with a second manufacturing facility in Bengaluru to serve domestic and export markets
2010
Commissioned new factories in Haridwar and Ahmedabad to meet growing demand across automotive and industrial segments
2011
Set up a Global Technical Centre in Bengaluru for advanced engineering and technology development

=== page 11 ===
the Power o F two:  
A strAtegic evolutionthe demerger story
Effective 1st October 2025, SKF India Limited entered a new 
phase of value creation by demerging its ‘Industrial’ business. 
This shift established two independent, purpose-built entities: SKF India (Industrial) Limited and SKF India Limited.
Building on a 102-year legacy in India, this move sharpens our strategic focus, 
enhances our operational agility, and positions each of our businesses to lead  within its specific market landscape.
Tailoring sector-specific strategies to meet unique market requirements
Strengthening capital discipline through business-aligned investment frameworks
Improving responsiveness via agile, application-focussed operating models
Enhancing transparency with clear performance benchmarksIndustrial
Anchored in infrastructure and manufacturing, the Industrial business serves sectors such as railways, renewable energy (wind), cement, mining, and heavy industries. It is driven by lifecycle-led demand, strong aftermarket intensity, and a relentless focus on reliability and long-term asset performance.
Automotive (SKF Vertevo)
Positioned at the centre of India’s mobility transformation, the Automotive business focusses on electrification, premiumisation, hybrid technologies, and  last-mile mobility. It operates in a space defined by rapid innovation cycles, platform shifts, evolving regulations, and changing consumer expectations.
Shareholder Continuity
To ensure continuity of ownership while enabling participation in both growth journeys, shareholders Focussed on Distinct 
Industry Requirements
The nation’s growth trajectory is 
being shaped by two powerful, yet fundamentally different engines: Industrial Expansion and Mobility Transformation. Each is characterised by distinct demand drivers, capital cycles, and innovation requirements. The demerger enables SKF to align with these divergent dynamics by:Two Independent 
Businesses, Clearer ExecutionFocussed Capital Allocation and Shareholder Continuity
To support these independent growth 
pathways, both entities have outlined focussed investment plans through 2030, ensuring capital efficiency and strengthened return ratios:

SKF’s journey in India 
began in 1923, laying the foundation for bringing advanced bearing technologies and engineering solutions to a rapidly industrialising economy. Over the decades, we have combined global technology leadership with deep local manufacturing and customer proximity.  Today, SKF India (Industrial) Limited continues this legacy as a focussed entity, leveraging the demerger to serve the evolving needs of India’s industrial ecosystem.

SKF Culture Wheel
Culture is beyond just our values. It encompasses 
many aspects such as our ability to learn, transformation capabilities, and embedding our purpose and values. 
The SKF Culture Wheel is an important part of the 
Purpose activation and overall drive to transform SKF’s culture towards being a purpose-driven  organisation.
At SKF, we believe we are all leaders, and leaders are 
responsible for Purpose and Culture. 
Our Culture is underpinned by our Values and 
therefore the Culture Wheel focuses on a specific 
part of the Purpose statement linked to an underpinning Value.

Scaling for Tomorrow: The Pune Expansion
We are investing in Pune to further strengthen our manufacturing capability. Envisioned as a ‘Factory of the Future’, the upcoming facility will bring together advanced infrastructure, digital enablement, and scalable operations, enabling us to expand capacity and improve operational efficiency. More than an expansion, it marks the next step in our journey of excellence, positioning us to support India’s accelerating industrial transformation with speed, reliability, and sustained performance.

engineering solutions 
For critic Al indus
tries segments in Focus
We operate across industries that are central to economic growth and industrial progress, 
where the nature of operations makes performance critical and leaves little room for error. Their varied demands call for more than standard solutions. Through application expertise and a solutions-led approach, we address our customer requirements with precision, staying aligned with their evolving needs while supporting productivity, reliability, and competitiveness.
Solutions and Applications
We provide bearings, seals, lubrication systems, and condition monitoring 
solutions for wind turbines and related renewable energy equipment. These solutions improve reliability, efficiency, and lifecycle performance in demanding environments.
Segment Potential
Renewable energy continues to gain relevance as industries increasingly focus on cleaner and more sustainable power sources.
SKF Edge
We apply application-specific technologies that increase uptime, reduce maintenance needs, and support efficient energy generation.
Renewable (Energy)
Solutions and Applications
We serve the metals industry with solutions for foundries, converters, casters, and rolling mills, designed to improve equipment reliability and productivity in harsh operating conditions.
Segment Potential
Metals remain essential, supporting manufacturing, infrastructure, and broader industrial value chains.
SKF Edge
Through robust bearing solutions and engineering expertise, we reduce downtime, optimise processes, and improve productivity.
Metals
Solutions and Applications
We cater to heavy industries with bearings, lubrication systems, seals, and reliability services that enhance performance and extend asset life under high-load conditions.
Segment Potential
Heavy industries remain integral to industrial development, infrastructure creation, and core economic activity.
SKF Edge
We deliver engineering-led solutions that improve uptime, reduce friction, and strengthen operational efficiency.
Heavy Industries

=== page 23 ===
Building A stronger 
indus
triAl sKF Br And strAtegic imPerAtive
Our strategy for FY 2025-26 is centred on 
strengthening our position as a trusted industrial partner in the Indian industry. We combine deep localisation, sharper regional responsiveness, strong customer focus, robust digital capabilities, with a strong people-led culture of innovation to deliver sustainable and profitable growth.
As India’s industrial landscape evolves towards higher 
efficiency, reliability, and sustainability, we bring together SKF’s global technology leadership with locally relevant solutions that address specific operating conditions, customer needs, and growth opportunities.
Localisation remains a strategic priority for SKF India (Industrial). We continue to enhance local manufacturing, sourcing, and engineering capabilities to improve supply-chain resilience, cost efficiency, and responsiveness to customers.
By increasing local manufacturing and strengthening 
domestic supplier ecosystems, we ensure continuity of supply in a dynamic geopolitical environment, while maintaining global SKF quality standards. Local application engineering and design capabilities enable us to develop products and solutions tailored to Indian operating conditions, industry requirements, and regulatory environments. This approach supports customers across key industrial segments such as power, metals, cement, mining, railways, and renewables.Localisation:  
Building India-Centric Value Chains
India’s industrial markets are regionally diverse, with 
distinct customer needs and industry dynamics. Our customer-centric approach focuses on empowering regional teams with greater autonomy, technical depth, and decision-making agility.
By strengthening regional sales, service, and 
application engineering presence, we enhance proximity to customers and enable faster response times. Regional insights help tailor solution offerings, service models, and go-to-market strategies, allowing us to remain relevant, competitive, and customer-centric across geographies.Customer Centricity:  
Closer Engagement, Faster Decisions1
2

We delivered revenue
from operations ofINR 34,403.6 Millionyear-to-date,including INR 9,457.2Million in the fourthquarter, reflectingquarter-on-quartergrowth of 9.8%. Thisperformance wassupported by thestrong commercialmomentum and theresilience of ouroperating model.Dear Stakeholders,
FY 2025-26 marked the beginning 
of a transformative new chapter for SKF India (Industrial) Limited. Following our successful demerger and subsequent listing, we now operate as a sharply focussed industrial enterprise, structurally aligned with the markets we serve and the customers we support. We delivered revenue from operations of INR 34,403.6 million year-to-date, including INR 9,457.2 million in the fourth quarter, reflecting quarter-on-quarter growth of 9.8%. This performance was supported by the strong commercial momentum and the resilience of our operating model.
Strategic Alignment in a 
Changing Landscape 
Global markets continue to be 
shaped by structural shifts that are no longer temporary disruptions. Geopolitical realignments, supply chain restructuring, and cost pressures represent a new operating reality that demands strategic adaptation rather than tactical responses.
At the same time, India continues 
to invest steadily in infrastructure and manufacturing, supported by strong domestic demand. With GDP growth estimated at 7.6% and a record capital expenditure outlay of INR 12.2 Trillion in the Union Budget 2026-27, India continues to strengthen its position as one of the world’s fastest-growing major economies. 
These developments are creating 
significant opportunities across sectors such as metals, renewable energy, railways, mining, cement, industrial automation and general machinery, wherein SKF Industrial remains well positioned to support this phase of manufacturing and industrial growth. 
Performance Rooted in 
Balance and Discipline
Our growth during the year was 
broad-based and well-diversified, reflecting the strength of our portfolio approach:
 OEM contributed 51% of sal
es, demonstrating deep 
integration with India’s manufacturing ecosystem
 Distribution contributed 35%, re
flecting strengthened 
market reach and improved channel effectiveness Railways, heavy industries, wind energy
, and general machinery 
all delivered steady traction across end-user segments 
This balance reflects resilience across market volatility and stems from a stable, predictable operating model that supports sustainable long-term growth.
Margins for the year reflected  
one-time transition costs associated with the demerger process. However, underlying operating performance remained stable, supported by disciplined execution, favourable portfolio mix, and sustained demand across key sectors. As transition-related costs normalise, the structural improvements in our earnings quality will become increasingly evident.
Demerger — Enabling 
Sharper Focus and Execution
The demerger of the Industrial 
business became effective on 1
st October, 2025, followed by 
the successful listing of SKF India (Industrial) Limited on 5th December, 2025, marking the beginning of a new phase as an independent industrial enterprise. The demerger represents far more than organisational restructuring. It is a fundamental shift in how we operate, allocate capital, and respond to market opportunities.
As a dedicated industrial company, 
we now benefit from:
 Greater clarity in capital all
ocation, directing 
investment towards highest-value opportunities within our industrial portfolio

Following the demerger, we plan to invest in a world class manufacturing facility in Pune. Additionally, we plan to strengthen the industrial platform with enhanced digital capabilities and application specific innovation. These investments may moderate margins in the near term while laying the foundation for long-term value creation. However, we remain focused on improving the margin profile through improvements in manufacturing and sourcing efficiencies, deepening localisation, pricing discipline and expanding sales of solutions.
Building Competitive 
Advantage Where It Matters Most
Our strategy in the region is centred 
on deep customer engagement, greater local presence, and the  co-creation of solutions that address critical industrial needs and support shared growth. Localisation as an Operational 
Advantage
During the year, we made 
meaningful progress in expanding domestic manufacturing capabilities across key product lines and applications. Advanced manufacturing channels were commissioned in the Pune factory for medium deep-groove ball bearings above 150 mm OD and UC-range ball bearing units. Overall localisation levels reached approximately 60%, with local procurement reaching approximately 78% during the year. 
Our commitment to the region 
is underscored by planned investments of INR 8,000-9,500 Million through 2030 towards the ‘Factory of the Future’ initiative in Pune. This will create a  future-ready manufacturing ecosystem. Localisation at SKF India (Industrial) extends well beyond cost competitiveness. It improves supply reliability, reduces lead times, deepens customer integration, and reduces exposure to global supply chain volatility.
The direction is clear—build a 
supply base that is closer to the customer and less dependent on external volatility. Over the coming years, we are targeting localisation of up to 75% of our portfolio, supported by the investments outlined above. Technology-led Differentiation
Innovation at SKF is focussed on 
combining technology with our advanced product development, engineering, simulations together with digital solutions to solve critical customer challenges. Recent launches across India and globally, including energy-efficient bearings, application-specific sealed solutions and magnetic bearing technologies, reflect this direction. Together, these innovations improve uptime, extend equipment life, and reduce lifecycle cost, strengthening our ability to deliver differentiated value in demanding industrial applications.
Digital initiatives such as 
intelligent product selector tools and AI-enabled condition monitoring solutions are further improving responsiveness, application accuracy, and customer engagement. The result is higher energy efficiency, fewer interventions, higher reliability under stress, and lower total cost of ownership, making this a 
sustainable competitive advantage.
From Products to Lifecycle 
Outcomes
The industrial market is undergoing 
a fundamental shift in what customers value. Expectations are evolving beyond component supply to enhanced uptime, reliability, and lifecycle performance. This plays to our strengths in core engineering and shapes how we are building  SKF India (Industrial) for the next phase of growth. 
By combining our product portfolio 
with engineering expertise, condition monitoring systems, lubrication solutions, and digital capabilities, we are positioning The demerger of the Industrial business

followed by the successful listing of SKF India (Industrial) Limited on 5

converting Agility into 
indus
triAl reliABility oPerAtion Al disciPline And Per Form Ance
At SKF India (Industrial), engineering strength and disciplined execution define our 
operational identity. Following the demerger, we realigned our manufacturing and supply chain priorities to be application-led and responsive. This strategic recalibration ensures our technical rigour results in the high-performance reliability and precision our industrial partners demand.
Aligning Production with Industrial Demand
Manufacturing Excellence
We are reshaping our 
manufacturing footprint to reflect specific industrial demands. Since product requirements vary by application and operating condition, our production systems are now organised based on application mapping. This ensures our manufacturing lines directly reflect the needs of sectors such as general machinery, agriculture, tractors, and electrical motors. Bearings above 150 mm OD continue to be supported through SKF’s global network, allowing our domestic footprint to remain focussed and efficient.
At the line level, we have enhanced 
our capabilities to support greater flexibility. We have established dedicated channels for specific applications, including tapered roller bearings (TRBs) for agriculture and specialised low-noise solutions for electrical motors. Our production channels accommodate flexible heat treatment, customised roller designs, and faster reset times, enabling us to manage short-batch, high-variation industrial demand.
The industries we serve play a 
critical role in moving society forward by producing essential goods, generating employment, and supporting local communities. As part of this ecosystem, we continue to advance innovation and operational efficiency while working towards more responsible and sustainable industrial practices.
ANNUAL REPORT 2025-26

=== page 38 ===
Building the Next Manufacturing Foundation
‘Factory of the Future’
To support long-term Industrial growth, we are investing in a next-generation 
manufacturing facility in Pune. Designed as a ‘Factory of the Future’, this facility integrates manufacturing, solutions, and supply chain capabilities into a single, scalable platform.
We have structured this investment to optimise cost and speed by leveraging 
channel transfers from within India and from SKF’s global network. This approach allows us to build capacity faster while maintaining capital discipline. Our investment decisions factor in critical risks such as regulatory compliance and capacity constraints. Delays in capacity creation could impact operational flexibility, limit export opportunities, and constrain our ability to scale industrial operations.

Our approach to research and development is shaped by the 
evolving demands of industrial applications, where operating conditions, performance requirements, and customer expectations vary significantly across sectors. Innovation is driven by application-specific challenges, field conditions, and the need to deliver reliable, efficient, and fit-for-purpose solutions across diverse industrial environments.
We operate within structured development and validation 
processes, where products and solutions are tested against demanding operating parameters, application requirements, and performance standards. Our engineering focus remains centred on reliability, operational efficiency, noise reduction, durability, energy optimisation, and enhanced equipment performance to support productivity and uptime across industrial operations.
Responding to Customer 
Challenges
Our application engineering 
teams proactively engage when customers face critical performance issues such as premature failures, extreme operating conditions, and uptime-sensitive operations. These scenarios often involve high loads, contamination, moisture, temperature variation, or inefficiencies arising from friction and misalignment.
We also support non-standard and 
legacy systems where catalogue products are insufficient, working closely with product development teams to deliver tailored engineering solutions.
Leveraging Global Platforms 
with Local Insight
We combine global engineering 
platforms with strong local expertise to deliver solutions aligned with real operating conditions. Our global capabilities include advanced bearing technologies, sealing systems, lubrication solutions, and simulation tools, supported by standardised methodologies.
This is complemented by our deep 
local understanding of customer processes, duty cycles, machine configurations, and competitive landscapes, ensuring solutions are both technically robust and operationally relevant. 
Integrating Field Feedback 
into Design
We maintain a continuous feedback 
loop between field performance and product development. Learnings from critical failures are captured through structured root cause and failure analysis, while competitive developments are tracked and integrated into R&D priorities.
This ensures that our solutions 
remain aligned with evolving performance expectations and emerging industry requirements.Delivering Measurable 
Application Impact

delivers tangible improvements across key performance areas:

At SKF India (Industrial) Limited, environmental responsibility is an 
operational mandate; optimising energy, resources, and waste drives long-term efficiency and reliability. Post-demerger, we institutionalised operational ownership across our manufacturing and entire value chain. By integrating sustainability from responsible sourcing to extending product life, we reduce our footprint while enhancing customer uptime, ensuring resilience and continuity as the business landscape evolves.
Our Approach
Our environmental strategy is 
guided by three pillars, ensuring our efforts deliver measurable outcomes and drive long-term value creation.
Climate 
Transformation
Circularity
Responsible 
Business
ANNUAL REPORT 2025-26 54
=== page 58 ===
Pillar 1: Climate TransformationAccelerating Decarbonisation across the Value Chain
Climate transformation remains a primary mandate, covering our entire footprint from supplier 
emissions to product lifecycle. We are committed to achieving decarbonisation by 2030 and Net Zero emissions by 2050.
Energy Efficiency
We aim for a 5% annual reduction in energy consumption. We are 
strengthening these efforts through SCADA-based monitoring and a focus on high-consumption areas like compressors, HVAC, and heat treatment. Additionally, by adopting technologies such as magnetic bearing chillers, heat recovery systems, and EC+ motors, we have 
identified opportunities to save approximately 8,000 MWh.
Renewable Energy Transition
We are increasing our use of renewable energy through onsite/offsite 
sourcing, Power Purchase Agreements (PPAs) and IREC mechanisms. 
Today, 97% of our energy is derived from renewable sources.
Fossil Fuel Elimination
We are phasing out LPG, CNG, and diesel, with a target to complete 
by 2029. This includes electrifying heat treatment processes and optimising energy systems.
Upstream
Engaging key steel and direct material suppliers to achieve a 32% 
emission reduction by 2030 through increased scrap usage and renewable-powered production.
DownstreamImproving logistics efficiency through better load utilisation, CNG 
vehicles, and localisation to reduce air freight. Additionally, we are targeting a 35% reduction in logistics emissions by 2030.Site-Level Progress
Decarbonisation Efforts 
beyond OperationsDecarbonised Operations
Our sites have achieved verified decarbonised status. We had 11 
energy-saving projects delivered 3,010 MWh of annual savings and phased out LPG usage in multiple furnaces. Additionally, the installation of a high-tension electrical feeder and the full electrification of heat treatment furnaces have effectively eliminated diesel reliance.
Operational TransformationAt our sites across India, we are driving significant Scope 1 
reductions. All our furnaces are electrified and we are also eliminating LPG used for atmosphere generation through N
2 purging.
*Industrial sites considered based on the delivery ratio for industrial production

=== page 69 ===
Our Commitment to Zero Harm
We are guided by a clear vision of Zero Injury, where the safety, health, and wellbeing of our 
employees, contractors, and business partners remain non-negotiable. Our approach is anchored in a structured, people-centric, and system-driven EHS framework, aligned with SKF Group standards and statutory requirements.
During FY 2025-26, we strengthened this framework through focussed interventions that enhance 
leadership accountability, employee capability, and operational discipline, while reinforcing a culture where safety is embedded into everyday decision-making.Safety and Wellbeing
ANNUAL REPORT 2025-26
66

=== page 70 ===
Driving Compliance and 
Governance
We maintain a robust compliance 
framework supported by regular monitoring and governance mechanisms.
Our approach includes periodic 
internal audits and third-party assessments, ensuring adherence to defined standards. EHS performance is tracked through structured indicators, with corrective actions closely monitored for timely closure.
Weekly cross-location EHS 
pulse reviews and governance 
forums enable continuous oversight, ensuring consistency in implementation while allowing flexibility to address site-specific  risks.
Proactive Risk Management
We adopt a prevention-first approach to workplace safety, focusing on early identification and mitigation of risks.
This includes routine identification 
and closure of unsafe acts and conditions, supported by structured reporting and follow-up mechanisms. We conduct machine safety risk assessments and implement engineering control upgrades to reduce exposure to hazards.
Our system also emphasises  
near-miss reporting and structured incident investigations, enabling us to capture learnings and prevent recurrence. Safety risk reviews are integrated into management forums, ensuring visibility and accountability at all levels.
Building Capability and 
Preparedness
Training and awareness remain 
central to our safety strategy.
We ensure mandatory safety 
induction programmes for all employees and contractors, supported by role-specific training covering machine safety, material handling, working at height, firefighting, and first aid.
To strengthen engagement, we 
deploy digital EHS learning 
modules and regular toolbox talks, reinforcing safe practices on the shopfloor.
We also conduct periodic 
emergency drills, covering fire response and loss prevention scenarios, ensuring preparedness and quick response in case of incidents.
Supporting Health and 
Wellbeing
We take a holistic view of safety, 
recognising the strong link between physical safety, mental health, and overall organisational performance.
Our efforts focus on creating safe 
and healthy working conditions that prevent occupational  ill-health, while supporting employee wellbeing. This integrated approach ensures that safety extends beyond compliance to encompass the overall quality of the work environment.Strengthening a Culture of 
Safety
We continued to advance our  
multi-year EHS culture transformation roadmap, with emphasis on leadership engagement, behavioural safety, and continuous improvement.
We conducted safety culture pulse 
assessments across locations to identify site-specific gaps and prioritise targeted interventions. To strengthen capability, we expanded our Safety Skill Centres, incorporating simulation-based training modules focussed on critical risk areas such as finger injury prevention and PPE effectiveness.
Leadership involvement remained 
central to driving this culture. Regular leadership-led safety 
talks, safety bulletins, and recognition platforms reinforced accountability and encouraged proactive participation.
We also marked key events such 
as National Safety Day, Safety 
Week, and Safety Month, engaging employees, contract partners, and union representatives through structured training programmes, safety walks, and kaizen initiatives focused on eliminating unsafe conditions and behaviours.
Moving Forward
We remain committed to strengthening our safety culture through continuous improvement, deeper engagement, and sustained leadership focus. By embedding safety into every aspect of our operations, we aim to create a workplace where every individual returns home safe, every day.

Refining Focus Post-demerger
With the formation of SKF India (Industrial) Limited in October 2025, 
we undertook a comprehensive review to align our initiatives more closely with our ‘Industrial’ operations and stakeholder expectations. Based on community assessments and consultations, we identified key priorities: water availability, sustainable livelihoods, natural resource management, and youth employability in industrial regions.
Strengthening Core Focus Areas
We have realigned our strategy to focus on our sites where we have a strong Industrial presence. Our efforts are now concentrated on three areas:
WeGyaan initiative has been expanded into a comprehensive Digital and Financial Literacy Programme, empowering rural communities as an integral part of the rural development framework.Initiatives linked to ‘Automotive’ objectives, including YES centres in Kolkata, Jaitaran, and Jagannathpur, were transferred to automotive entity. We continue to support skill development relevant to industrial sectors, including training for youth in job roles such as shop floor technicians, maintenance, operators, and logistics among others.

Identified as a key enabling 
theme for the future. Although not active during FY 2025-26, it will leverage our engineering expertise to support industry-linked vocational training for youth in our operating regions.

Impact Measurement
Each programme is engineered 
with a clear theory of change and defined KPIs to move beyond simple activity tracking toward objective results. Progress is monitored through data-driven systems and periodic field assessments that provide direct, on-ground insights into our initiatives. For long-term programmes, independent third-party evaluations are conducted to rigorously measure transformative outcomes, specifically targeting improvements in livelihoods, water security, and environmental resilience.
Oversight
The CSR Board Committee 
approves plans and reviews progress, while a dedicated CSR team handles execution, partner selection, and rigorous due diligence to ensure compliance and transparency. This governance is strengthened by standardised processes for partner evaluation and reporting, supported by  KPI-based tracking and digital systems. Furthermore, regular monitoring through monthly and quarterly reviews, alongside independent assessments and long-term planning, ensures sustained accountability and the continuity of our initiatives.Moving Forward

2. State of Company’s Affairs:
 The Company w
as incorporated on 17th December, 
2024 with a vision to empower global enterprises through advanced, future-ready technologies designed to address the needs of the next decade. SKF India (Industrial) Limited is an affiliate of the Sweden-based SKF Group, which was founded in 1907. SKF Group started its operations in India in 1923 and it is primarily engaged in the manufacturing and supply of high-quality bearings and their components in India, serving diverse industrial and automotive applications.
 The Company op
erates as a technology-driven 
organisation, committed to delivering innovative, efficient, and sustainable engineering solutions. Its product and service portfolio is built around an integrated technology approach encompassing bearings and units, seals, mechatronics, lubrication systems, and allied services.
 Through 
its solutions, the Company aims to support 
customers in enhancing operational performance by reducing friction, improving energy efficiency, and increasing equipment reliability and longevity. The Company places strong emphasis on research-led innovation and continuous improvement, enabling it to offer customised, value-added solutions tailored to specific customer and industry requirements.
 With a customer-
centric approach and focus on 
sustainability, the Company is well-positioned to contribute to the advancement of the industrial sectors while creating long-term value for all its stakeholders.

Directors’ Report (contd.)
Demerger of Industrial business of SKF India 
Limited into the Company
 Your
 Company was incorporated on 17th December, 
2024, as a wholly owned subsidiary of SKF India Limited for the purpose of carrying on the Industrial business, as part of SKF Group’s global separation of its Automotive and Industrial businesses. 
 The Boar
d of Directors of the Company and SKF India 
Limited, at their respective meetings held on 26th 
December, 2024, approved the Scheme of Arrangement between SKF India Limited (‘Demerged Company’) and SKF India (Industrial) Limited (‘Resulting Company’) and their respective shareholders and creditors (‘Scheme’) for the demerger of Industrial Business (as defined in the Scheme) into the Company on a going concern basis, subject to requisite statutory and regulatory approvals under Sections 230-232 of the Companies Act, 2013.
 The Demerger
 was undertaken to inter-alia enable 
the Demerged Company and the Resulting Company to operate independently, pursue distinct growth strategies, improve efficiency, enhance strategic flexibility, de-risk both the Automotive and Industrial businesses, and unlock value for shareholders while providing clearer visibility into each business’s performance. 
 The Scheme was sanctioned b
y the Hon’ble National 
Company Law Tribunal, Mumbai Bench vide its Order dated 26
th September, 2025. The Appointed Date and 
Effective Date of the Scheme is 1st October, 2025.
 Changes in Shar
e Capital of Company
 Authorise
d share capital:
 The members appro
ved the increase in authorised 
capital of the Company from 150,000 (One Lakh Fifty Thousand) equity shares of INR 10/- (Rupees Ten only) each aggregating to INR 1,500,000/- (Rupees Fifteen Lakh only) to 50,000,000 (Five Crores) equity shares of INR 10/- (Rupees Ten only) each aggregating to  
INR 500,000,000/- (Rupees Fifty Crores only) vide its Extra Ordinary General Meeting held on 20
th May, 
2025 by passing an Ordinary Resolution. 
 Paid-up Shar
e Capital:
 Upon the Scheme be
coming effective, on 17th October, 
2025, Company allotted 49,437,963 fully paid-up equity shares of INR 10/- each to the members of SKF India Limited as on the Record Date (i.e 15
th October, 
2025), in accordance with the share entitlement ratio of 1 (One) fully paid-up equity share of INR 10/- (Indian Rupees Ten only) each of the Resulting Company for every 1 (One) fully paid-up equity share of INR 10/- (Indian Rupees Ten only) each held in the Demerged Company (‘Share Entitlement Ratio’), as set out in the Scheme. The entire pre-scheme share capital of INR MINR 0.1 of the Company held by SKF India Limited was cancelled pursuant to the Scheme. The Company ceased to be SKF India Limited’s wholly owned subsidiary.

12.
 Safety
/Sustainability Safety :
 a) Safety:
  SKF is c
ommitted to a vision of “Zero-Injury” 
for everyone working at its sites and for those engaged off-site. Safety is our foremost concern, and we firmly believe that all work-related incidents are preventable.
  Health and Saf
ety Management continues 
to be the Company’s top priority, with safety embedded in our strategy and daily operations. We place strong emphasis on the welfare of our employees, ensuring a safe and healthy environment free from injuries, accidents, and occupational health hazards. 
  To support th
is commitment, SKF has adopted 
a structured approach in EHS management system where we implement safety Policies and programmes that integrate safety into business processes, driving continual improvements in safety performance.Directors’ Report (contd.)
SKF India (Industrial) Limited 85
Financial 
StatementsStatutory ReportsCorporate Overview
=== page 89 ===
  Our safety strategy is anchored around 3 focus 
areas:
  1. Safety 
Pyramid
  2. Machine 
Safety 
  3. Contin
ual Improvement 
  SKF is certified t
o ISO 45001:2018, ensuring 
compliance with the standards. Our aim is to set 
benchmarks that exceed certified management systems, and we actively share our safety standards and experience with operators, contractors, and professional organisations. Safety risks are managed across all business units using robust standards, controls, and compliance systems, with particular focus on the use of the “Safety Pyramid” to drive proactive reporting and following up on first aids, near-miss, unsafe condition and unsafe behaviour. Lessons learnt are shared across units for horizontal deployment.
  Structured pr
ograms are maintained to 
strengthen a robust safety culture throughout our factories, Sales Offices, Warehouses, and project sites. SKF has kick-start on the machine safety “investment-ready concept” programme in which no new investment is approved unless mandatory management training on introduction to machine safety is fulfilled and minimum 3 machine risk assessments to be conducted by qualified machine safety experts. Consequently, the ambition is to have year-on-year improvement in the number of risk assessments conducted according to ISO machine standards and corrective actions taken for all our machines.
  We hav
e begun mapping employees’ safety 
competency to identify areas for improvement and enhanced skills and knowledge through our KUSHAL Training Centre. Recently, the inauguration of the “Safety Skill Center” at our Pune site further reinforces the importance of workplace safety and our goal of achieving zero injuries. Training and awareness remain central to our safety strategy, and our EHS practices and policies are communicated to contractors and suppliers, ensuring alignment with our safety requirements.
  In add
ition, SKF commemorates National 
Safety Month and World Environment Day at all sites, actively engaging stakeholders in these initiatives.  The Company has reported no recordable accident.  
The continued positive trend in reporting unsafe conditions, behaviors, near-miss and first aid incidents underscores our increased vigilance and responsiveness to safety risks.
  SKF prioritize
s safety in every business decision, 
ensuring that employees’ health and well-being remain at the core of its operations.

13. Interna
l Controls with Respect to Financial 
Statements:
 The Company has pr
oper and adequate policies 
and procedures in place. These procedures ensure reliability and efficient conduct of business. Periodic review and control mechanisms ensure the effectiveness and adequacy of the internal control systems that the Company operates in. Additionally, it views internal audit as a vital part of management control systems.
 It helps keep the manag
ement informed about the 
existence and efficacy of the control systems and processes in the organisation.
 The management has impl
emented an effective 3 
(three) lines of defence to monitor controls – first at the Management level, second by implementing an 
effective internal control system monitored by the Internal Controls team and, third by Internal Audits.
 The Company
, during the year, reviewed its Internal 
Financial Control (IFC) systems. It continually worked towards establishing a more robust and effective IFC framework. Being part of the SKF Group, the Company adheres to SICS (SKF Internal Control Standards). This is a customised control system required to be adhered to, across the globe, by all SKF companies. The standards specified by SICS are an integral part of the standard operating procedures for all business functions.
 A grea
t extent of emphasis is placed on having 
compensating controls within the process, minimising deviations and exceptions. The Internal Controls team verifies the existence of adequate controls and test them. The Internal Audit function conducts Process Audits.
 The Company a
lso undergoes periodic audits by 
specialised external professional firms. Risks/improvement areas, identified in the audits, are reviewed and mitigation plans are put in place. The status of implementation of action plans for major observations is submitted to every Audit Committee for review.
 The Audit 
Committee reviews reports submitted by 
the management and audit reports submitted by Internal and Statutory Auditors. The Audit Committee also meets Statutory Auditors to ascertain, inter alia, their views on the adequacy of internal control systems. Based on the Committee’s evaluation, it was concluded that as of 31
st March, 2026, the internal 
financial controls were adequate and operating effectively.
 The Company has c
omplied with the specific 
requirements as laid out under Section 134(5)(e) of the Companies Act, 2013. It calls for the establishment and implementation of an Internal Financial Control framework that supports compliance with the requirements of the Act concerning the Director’s Responsibility Statement. Adequacy of controls of the processes is also being reviewed by the Internal Controls function. Suggestions to further strengthen the processes are shared with the respective process owners. Any significant findings, along with management response and status of action plans, are periodically shared with and reviewed by the Audit Committee.Directors’ Report (contd.)

18. Vigil Mechanism/Wh istle-blower Policy:
 Your
 Company has framed a Vigil Mechanism Policy 
in confirmation with Section 177(9) of the Act and 
Regulation 22 of SEBI LODR wherein the employees are free to report any improper activity resulting in violation of laws, rules, regulations or code of conduct by any of the employees directly to the Chairperson of the Audit Committee besides others. The Board’s Audit Committee oversees the functioning of this policy. The Audit Committee periodically reviews the existence and functioning of the mechanism. It reviews the status of complaints received under this policy on a quarterly basis.

Global Economic Overview
The global economy is entering 
a more uncertain phase, with growth moderating below earlier expectations. According to the International Monetary Fund (IMF) April 2026 update, global GDP growth is now projected at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.2% in 2027, reflecting a softer trajectory compared to prior estimates. This moderation comes despite the resilience shown in recent years and signals a transition towards a slower, more constrained growth environment.This shift is being shaped by a combination of factors. Escalating geopolitical disruptions, particularly the conflict in the Middle East, rising trade fragmentation, and tighter financial conditions are weighing on global activity. Commodity price volatility and firming inflation expectations are adding further pressure, particularly for emerging economies dependent on imports. 
At the same time, structural 
drivers continue to support the global economy. Investment in productivity-enhancing technologies, especially artificial intelligence and digitalisation, remains a key medium-term growth lever. However, the pace and distribution of these gains remain uneven across regions and sectors.
Growth Dynamics Across Regions
Growth trends continue to diverge across economies:
 Advanced economies are expe
cted to grow at 1.8% 
in 2026 and 1.7% in 2027, supported by stabilising inflation and improving real incomes, but constrained by high interest rates, fiscal consolidation, and demographic pressures.  Emerging markets and de
veloping economies (EMDEs) 
are projected to grow at 3.9% in 2026, before recovering to 4.2% in 2027, remaining the primary engines of global growth. However, they face heightened vulnerability to external shocks, capital flow volatility, and commodity price movements. Notably, growth pressures are expected to be more pronounced in EMDEs in the near term, particularly in commodity-importing nations with limited policy buffers.
Inflation and Financial Conditions
The global disinflation trend is expected to pause temporarily. Inflation may rise modestly in 2026 before resuming its downward trajectory in 2027, driven by commodity price movements and geopolitical developments. 
Financial conditions are gradually 
easing but remain tighter than pre-pandemic levels. Elevated borrowing costs continue to influence investment decisions, corporate financing, and consumer demand across markets.Economic Overview
ANNUAL REPORT 2025-26
96

=== page 100 ===
Trade and 
Geopolitical Context
Global trade is undergoing 
structural realignment. While supply chains have become more diversified, fragmentation and  policy-driven shifts continue to reshape trade flows.
Geopolitical developments 
are emerging as a central risk factor. Conflicts and strategic tensions are impacting energy markets, logistics networks, and technology ecosystems. Rising defence spending is also creating macroeconomic trade-offs, supporting short-term activity but adding to fiscal pressures and public debt over the medium term.

Outlook
The global outlook remains 
stable but tilted to the downside, with growth moderating, inflation remaining uneven, and policy flexibility constrained by elevated public debt and limited fiscal space. Prolonged geopolitical conflicts, increasing trade fragmentation, slower-than-expected productivity gains from emerging technologies such as artificial intelligence, and the possibility of tightening global financial conditions continue to pose key risks to the economic environment. At the same time, there is potential for improvement if technological adoption accelerates or if trade tensions ease in a sustained manner. Overall, the operating landscape is characterised by moderate growth, heightened uncertainty, and ongoing structural transitions, requiring businesses to remain focused on resilience, disciplined capital allocation, and the ability to respond effectively to external disruptions.

Indian Economic Overview
In FY 2025-26, India sustained 
strong macroeconomic momentum despite an unsettled global environment. Growth remained anchored in domestic demand, infrastructure expansion, and manufacturing activity, even as external risks from geopolitical tensions and commodity price volatility increased. According to estimates from the Reserve Bank of India and national data sources, India’s real GDP is expected to grow at around 7.6%, supported by resilient services, steady consumption, and continued investment momentum.
For sectors linked to metals, 
mobility, and industrial applications, this environment translated into stable underlying demand, driven by infrastructure creation, automotive production, and energy transition initiatives. Policy emphasis on strengthening domestic manufacturing, reducing import dependencies, and building industrial capacity continues to support long-term growth visibility.

Domestic demand remained the 
primary growth engine. Stable private consumption, supported by improving income levels and earlier easing of inflation, combined with sustained public capital expenditure, continued to drive infrastructure creation and industrial activity.
Government-led investments 
in logistics corridors, industrial clusters, and urban infrastructure are strengthening demand across core sectors such as steel, automotive, and engineering. In parallel, initiatives focused on manufacturing competitiveness, supply chain localisation, and capacity expansion are reinforcing India’s position as a key global manufacturing hub.

Inflation and Policy Environment
Inflation trends reflected both 
domestic and global influences. While earlier moderation was supported by improved supply conditions, emerging pressures from rising energy and commodity prices have introduced upside risks. This has implications for input costs and margin management across material-intensive industries.
The Reserve Bank of India 
maintained a calibrated and cautious monetary stance, balancing inflation control with growth support. Headline inflation is expected to remain within a manageable range, although it remains sensitive to crude oil price movements, weather-related supply disruptions, and exchange rate volatility. Policy transmission continues to support liquidity conditions while ensuring macroeconomic stability.
External Sector and Financial Stability
India’s external position remained broadly stable, supported by strong services exports and resilient remittance inflows. However, rising crude oil prices and global uncertainties may widen the current account deficit and exert pressure on inflation and currency stability.
Despite these challenges, India 
continues to attract strong foreign investment flows, driven by its structural growth prospects, expanding industrial base, and ongoing ease-of-doing-business reforms. The financial system remains robust, with healthy credit growth, improved asset quality, and strong capital buffers across banks, supporting continued economic expansion.

Market

Bearings are evolving from standardised mechanical components to precision-engineered, performance-critical systems, improving uptime, reliability and energy efficiency in increasingly complex operating environments.

This is increasing demand for bearings capable of operating at higher speeds and load capacities while maintaining reliability and durability.Shift Towards Higher-Speed, 
Higher-Load and Compact  Designs

Outlook
The medium-term outlook for the Indian industrial bearings market remains constructive, supported by improving capacity utilisation across industries, continued capital investment and increasing emphasis on reliability and lifecycle performance. Competitive dynamics are expected to be shaped by localisation strategies, technology differentiation and growing demand for value-added solutions and services. While pricing pressures and input cost volatility may persist, companies with strong engineering capabilities, customer alignment and operational agility are likely to be better positioned to capture opportunities as the market evolves.

India’s manufacturing sector 
continued to strengthen during FY 2025-26, supported by infrastructure spending, domestic demand and policy-led industrial expansion. Manufacturing output grew 8.1% in December 2025, while industrial GVA rose 7% during H1 FY 2025-26, led by automobiles, electronics, transport equipment and engineering industries. India’s manufacturing ecosystem is also steadily moving towards  higher-value and technology-intensive production, with medium- and high-technology industries contributing 46.3% of manufacturing value added.
Government initiatives such as 
Production-Linked Incentive (PLI) schemes, industrial corridor development and public infrastructure investments continue to support capacity creation and localisation across sectors. Public capital expenditure stood at  INR 11.21 Lakh Crores in  FY 2025-26, while private investment announcements rose significantly during the year, reflecting improving industrial confidence and supply chain diversification efforts.
The sector is also witnessing 
increasing adoption of automation, predictive maintenance, advanced machinery and digitally integrated operations to improve productivity, reliability and energy efficiency. These trends are strengthening 
demand for precision bearings, industrial services, condition monitoring and engineered reliability solutions across manufacturing industries.
For Industrial Bearings and

Rising focus on uptime, operational efficiency and localisation is also creating opportunities for value-added services, application engineering and predictive maintenance solutions.Key Downstream Industries 
Outlook
India’s manufacturing sector is expected to maintain long-term growth momentum, supported by infrastructure development, supply chain diversification, localisation and increasing industrial automation. While global trade and commodity volatility may create short-term fluctuations, sustained industrial investments are expected to support long-term demand for industrial bearings and reliability solutions.

Indian Railways
Indian Railways continues to be a 
key driver of India’s infrastructure and logistics ecosystem, supporting freight movement across coal, steel, cement, foodgrains and containers while enabling large-scale passenger mobility. Rising industrial activity, urbanisation and  corridor-based development are increasing the strategic importance of rail infrastructure. 
The Union Budget 2026-27 
allocated a record INR 2,78,000 Crores to Indian Railways, focused on network expansion, electrification, rolling stock procurement, station redevelopment and freight corridor development. High-speed rail projects and Dedicated Freight Corridors (DFCs) are further improving connectivity, logistics efficiency and freight capacity. 
Railway modernisation is also 
accelerating adoption of advanced signalling systems, digital asset monitoring, energy-efficient locomotives and automated train protection technologies. Increasing localisation and domestic manufacturing of railway components are supporting India’s broader industrial ecosystem. 
(Source: https://prsindia.org/files/budget/budget_parliament/2026/DfG_Analysis_2026-27-Railways.pdf 
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressNoteDetails.aspx?NoteId=157295&ModuleId=3&reg=3&lang=1)
Outlook
Indian Railways is expected to remain a long-term infrastructure growth driver, supported by sustained capital 
expenditure, freight expansion and technology-led modernisation. Continued investments in rolling stock, electrification and corridor development are expected to support steady demand for industrial bearings and railway reliability solutions.For Industrial Bearings and Services, expanding investments in rail infrastructure, rolling stock, freight corridors and high-speed rail are supporting demand for bearings, condition monitoring, lubrication and reliability solutions across passenger, freight and metro segments. Growth in rail freight and network modernisation is also expected to strengthen aftermarket and maintenance opportunities.

Capital Goods
The capital goods sector remains 
a key enabler of India’s industrial and infrastructure expansion, supporting manufacturing, construction, mining, energy and logistics. Rising infrastructure spending and industrial investments continue to strengthen demand for plant, machinery and engineering equipment. 
Government capital expenditure has 
increased significantly to INR 11.21 Lakh Crores in FY 2025-26, with INR 12.2 Lakh Crores proposed for FY 2026-27. Capital goods output grew 8.1% in December 2025, while capacity utilisation improved to around 74–75%, reflecting improving industrial activity and investment momentum. Domestic production reached INR 2,05,194 Crores in  FY 2024-25. 
Renewable Energy
Renewable energy remains central to India’s energy transition and industrial decarbonisation agenda. India’s renewable energy capacity increased from 76.38 GW in 2014 to 274.68 GW in March 2026, led by strong growth in solar and wind energy.
Wind energy continues to gain 
momentum, with India adding a record 6.05 GW of wind capacity during FY 2025-26 versus  4.15 GW in the previous year. Installed wind capacity increased to 56.09 GW in March 2026, while domestic wind turbine manufacturing capacity expanded to around 24 GW, supported by localisation initiatives and rising private-sector participation.Government initiatives such as the PLI scheme, container manufacturing incentives, construction equipment schemes and investments in precision manufacturing are further supporting localisation and technology adoption across industrial sectors. 
Rising investments in industrial 
machinery, construction equipment, automation and heavy engineering are expected to support demand for bearings, lubrication, condition monitoring and reliability solutions across OEM and aftermarket segments.
Government measures to accelerate 
project approvals, strengthen grid infrastructure and support domestic renewable manufacturing are further improving sector visibility and execution. Increasing renewable penetration is also driving investments in transmission, energy storage and grid modernisation.
For industrial bearing companies, 
expanding investments in wind energy, renewable infrastructure and turbine manufacturing are supporting demand for bearings, lubrication, condition monitoring and reliability solutions. Wind energy remains a key opportunity area, particularly in application-specific and high-performance industrial solutions.
Outlook
The capital goods sector is expected to maintain healthy growth, supported by infrastructure execution, manufacturing expansion and private sector investments. Increasing automation, localisation and technology-led manufacturing are expected to create sustained demand for industrial bearings and engineering reliability solutions.

Outlook
India’s renewable energy 
sector is expected to maintain strong long-term growth, supported by rising power demand, decarbonisation targets and continued policy support. Increasing wind installations, localisation and renewable infrastructure expansion are expected to support sustained demand for industrial bearings and engineering reliability solutions.

Company Overview
SKF India (Industrial) (also 
referred to as ‘The Company’) is a technology-driven industrial solutions provider dedicated to enabling reliable rotation, operational efficiency, and lifecycle performance across critical industries.
The Company was incorporated on 
17
th December, 2024, as a wholly 
owned subsidiary of SKF India Limited for the purpose of carrying on the industrial business, as part of SKF Group’s global separation of its Automotive and Industrial businesses. 
The Boards of Directors of the 
Company and SKF India Limited, at their respective meetings held on 26
th December, 2024, approved the 
Scheme of Arrangement between SKF India Limited (‘Demerged Company’) and SKF India (Industrial) Limited (‘Resulting Company’) and their respective shareholders and creditors (‘Scheme’) for the demerger of Industrial Business (as defined in the Scheme) into the Company on a going concern basis, subject to requisite statutory and regulatory approvals under Sections 230–232 of the Companies Act, 2013.
The Demerger was undertaken to 
inter alia enable the Demerged Company and the Resulting Company to operate independently, pursue distinct growth strategies, improve efficiency, enhance strategic flexibility, de-risk both the Automotive and Industrial businesses, and unlock value for shareholders while providing clearer visibility into each business’s performance. 
The Scheme was approved by the 
Hon’ble National Company Law Tribunal, Mumbai Bench vide its Order dated 26
th September, 2025. 
The Appointed Date and Effective Date of the Scheme are  1
st October, 2025.Upon the Scheme becoming effective, the Company allotted fully paid-up equity shares of INR 10/- each to the members of SKF India Limited as on the Record Date (i.e., 15
th October, 2025), in accordance 
with the share entitlement ratio of 1 (One) fully paid-up equity share of INR 10/- (Indian Rupees Ten only) each of the Resulting Company for every 1 (One) fully paid-up equity share of INR 10/- (Indian Rupees Ten only) each held in the Demerged Company (‘Share Entitlement Ratio’), as set out in the Scheme.
In terms of the Scheme, the 
Company’s shares were listed on BSE Limited (Scrip Code: 544572) and the National Stock Exchange of India Limited (Symbol: SKFINDUS) on 5
th December, 2025, 
marking its commencement as an independent listed entity positioned to pursue growth in the Industrial business segment.

Risk Management
In an increasingly complex 
and interconnected business environment, the Company recognises that effective risk management is essential to sustaining growth and protecting stakeholder value. The Company has adopted a structured Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) framework that enables a proactive, integrated and forward-looking approach to 
Enterprise Risk 
Management Process
The Company follows a 
structured and integrated Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) process to identify, assess, monitor and mitigate risks across the organisation. This process is embedded into business operations and strategic decision-making, enabling a proactive approach to risk management.identifying, assessing and mitigating risks across the organisation.
The ERM framework is embedded 
within the Company’s strategy, operations and governance processes. Risks are systematically identified across all functional areas, including manufacturing, supply chain, technology, finance, compliance and sustainability. These risks are evaluated based on their likelihood and potential impact, consolidated at an organisational level, and reviewed periodically by senior management. The Risk Management Committee provides oversight to ensure alignment with strategic priorities, regulatory requirements and governance standards.
Risk Identification and AssessmentRisk assessments are conducted across all business and functional areas. Each function identifies key risks relevant to its operations, covering strategic, operational, financial, regulatory and environmental aspects.
Risk Consolidation
Identified risks are consolidated by business and functional representatives. These risks are evaluated, prioritised and aligned based on their potential impact and likelihood. The consolidation process is facilitated by the compliance function to ensure consistency and completeness.
Management Review
The consolidated risk assessment is reviewed by the Company’s management. This review ensures that key risks are understood at an organisational level and that appropriate mitigation strategies are defined and implemented.

IT and Digital Infrastructure
Digital systems support SKF 
India (Industrial)’s manufacturing operations, supply chain coordination, and customer service delivery. Given the diversity of industrial applications, system integration and real-time data visibility are critical to maintaining process control, consistency, and responsiveness.
The Company is transitioning 
to SAP S/4HANA to establish a unified ERP backbone integrating manufacturing, supply chain, finance, and commercial functions. This enables standardised workflows, improved data integrity, and real-time reporting, reducing redundancies and strengthening cross-functional decision-making in the post-demerger structure.
Digitalisation is embedded within 
operations through connected manufacturing systems. IoT-enabled machines, MES platforms, digital twins, and AI-enabled inspection tools provide real-time visibility into production performance, improving planning accuracy, quality control, and responsiveness. Asset reliability is strengthened through condition monitoring systems, CMMS platforms such as eMaint, and energy management systems, enabling predictive maintenance, improved equipment availability, and reduced unplanned downtime. 
Supply chain operations are 
supported by warehouse and transport management systems, digital procurement tools, and visibility platforms, improving traceability, forecasting reliability, and inventory optimisation while reducing single-source dependency risks. 
Real-time monitoring, predictive 
analytics, and automated control systems enhance process discipline across manufacturing, maintenance, and supply chain functions, creating an integrated, data-driven operational ecosystem.
To strengthen internal capability, 
SKF Group has established a Global Competency Centre (GCC) in India, supporting development of tailored IT solutions, strengthening cybersecurity resilience, and enhancing enterprise systems capability across the organisation.
Customer-facing digital solutions 
remain a key differentiator. Tools such as Bearing Select enable application-specific product selection, while Microlog and IMx systems support real-time diagnostics and predictive maintenance, helping customers reduce downtime and optimise asset performance.
The Company is advancing  
AI-enabled analytics and reliability engineering tools to deliver insights into asset health, remaining useful life, and operational performance, supporting a shift towards  solutions-led and outcome-based services. 
In parallel, internal innovation 
platforms and collaborations with startups and academic institutions support the development and adoption of emerging digital technologies.

Disclosure of Accounting 
Treatment
The financial statements of SKF 
India (Industrial) Limited have been prepared in accordance with the applicable Accounting Standards prescribed under the Companies Act, 2013 and the relevant rules framed thereunder.
Wherever, in the preparation of 
the financial statements, any accounting treatment differs from that prescribed under an Accounting Standard, the Company discloses such deviation, if any, along with management’s explanation as to why the alternative treatment is considered more representative of the true and fair view of the underlying business transaction.
For the financial year ended 
31
st March, 2026, there were no 
departures from the prescribed Accounting Standards.
Internal Control Systems and 
Their Adequacy
The Company has established 
policies, procedures, and internal control systems commensurate with the size and nature of its operations. These systems are designed to ensure orderly conduct of business, safeguarding of assets, prevention and detection of fraud and errors, accuracy of accounting records and timely preparation of reliable financial information.
A structured three-line defence 
framework is in place, comprising operational management, the Internal Control function and Internal Audit. This framework ensures process-level accountability, independent oversight and periodic evaluation of control effectiveness. Additionally, continuous monitoring mechanisms support early identification of control gaps.
As part of the global SKF Group, the 
Company adheres to  
SKF Internal Control Standards (SICS), a harmonised framework governing key business processes. Standard operating procedures are aligned with SICS requirements, with emphasis on control discipline and minimising deviations and ensuring process discipline. Furthermore, control adequacy is periodically tested by the Internal Control team.
The Internal Audit function, 
supported by specialised external firms, conducts risk-based audits during the year. Audit observations and improvement areas are reviewed with management, and mitigation plans are implemented within defined timelines. The Audit Committee regularly reviews significant findings, management responses and the status of corrective actions.
The Audit Committee also reviews 
reports from statutory auditors and meets them periodically to assess the adequacy of internal control systems. Based on evaluation as of 31
st March, 2026, Internal Financial 
Controls were assessed as adequate and operating effectively.
The Company complies with the 
requirements of Section 134(5)(e) of the Companies Act, 2013, relating to the establishment and maintenance of an effective internal financial control framework in support of the Directors’ Responsibility Statement.

1. Background and Corporate History
 Your
 Company was incorporated on 17th December, 
2024, as a wholly owned subsidiary of SKF India 
Limited for the purpose of carrying on the Industrial business, as part of SKF Group’s global separation of its Automotive and Industrial businesses.
 The demerg
er was undertaken as part of a strategic 
reorganisation by the SKF Group to realign its India operations into two focused and independently governed businesses i.e., Automotive and Industrial. This decision was driven by the recognition that the two segments operate in distinct market ecosystems, cater to different customer bases, and are influenced by divergent growth drivers, capital requirements, and competitive dynamics.
 The Boar
d of Directors of the Company and SKF India 
Limited, at their respective meetings held on 26th 
December, 2024, approved the Scheme of Arrangement between SKF India Limited (‘Demerged Company’) and SKF India (Industrial) Limited (‘Resulting Company’) and their respective shareholders and creditors (‘Scheme’) for the demerger of Industrial Business (as defined in the Scheme) into the Company on a going concern basis, subject to requisite statutory and regulatory approvals under Sections 230-232 of the Companies Act, 2013.
 The Scheme rec
eived the requisite approvals 
from shareholders, creditors, stock exchanges, and regulatory authorities and was subsequently sanctioned by the Hon’ble National Company Law Tribunal (“NCLT”) on 26
th September, 2025. The 
Appointed Date and Effective Date of the Scheme is 1
st October, 2025.
 Upon the Scheme be
coming effective, on 17th October, 
2025, Company allotted 49,437,963 fully paid-up equity shares of INR 10/- each to the members of SKF India Limited as on the Record Date (i.e 15
th October, 
2025), in accordance with the share entitlement ratio of 1 (One) fully paid-up equity share of INR 10/- (Indian Rupees Ten only) each of the Resulting Company for every 1 (One) fully paid-up equity share of INR 10/- (Indian Rupees Ten only) each held in the Demerged Company (‘Share Entitlement Ratio’), as set out in Scheme. The entire pre-Scheme share capital of MINR 0.1 of the Company held by SKF India Limited was cancelled pursuant to the Scheme. The Company ceased to be SKF India Limited’s wholly owned subsidiary.
 In terms of the Scheme, C
ompany’s shares were listed 
on BSE Limited (Scrip Code: 544572) and the National Stock Exchange of India Limited (Symbol: SKFINDUS) on 5
th December, 2025, marking its commencement 
as an independent listed entity positioned to pursue growth in the Industrial business segment.
 The Company has p
ut in place the requisite governance 
framework, policies, committees of the Board, and reporting mechanisms to ensure compliance with the applicable listing and corporate governance requirements. For the financial year 2025–26, such compliances have been made from the date of listing to the extent applicable.

code of governance.
 SKF India (Industr
ial) Limited (‘SKF Industrial’ or

Strong leadership and effective corporate governance practices have been SKF’s hallmark inherited from the SKF Group culture and ethos. Over the decades, SKF has consistently demonstrated very principled conduct and has earned its reputation for trust and integrity while building a highly successful global business.

2.1
 Group Structur
e:
 SKF India 
(Industrial) Limited is an affiliate of the 
Sweden-based SKF Group, which was founded in 1907. SKF presently serves nearly all industries, including heavy industries, metals & mining, aerospace, railways, renewable energy, medical, food & beverage and more.
 SKF’s 
mission is to be the undisputed leader in the 
bearings business by offering solutions that reduce friction and CO
2 emissions, whilst at the same time 
increasing machine uptime and performance. Our products and services around the rotating shaft include bearings, seals, lubrication management, 
artificial intelligence and wireless condition monitoring.  
SKF works to reduce friction, make things run faster, longer, cleaner and more safely. By doing this in the most effective, productive and sustainable way SKF contributes to its vision of a world of reliable rotation. SKF works with its customers at every stage in their asset life cycle, providing solutions from design right through to maintenance and back to design upgrades. The Company’s business runs across different industry verticals, geographical markets and is global in nature.

 Governanc
e Structure:
 The corpor
ate governance standards established 
(and updated from time to time) by the Board of the Company provide a structure within which directors and the Management can effectively pursue the Company’s objectives for the benefit of its stakeholders.
 SKF Industria
l’s governance structure comprises the 
Board of Directors, Committees of the Board and the Management.
 The Board s
ets out the overall corporate objectives 
and provides direction and independence to the management to achieve organisational objectives for value creation through sustainable profitable growth. The Board and its Committees guide, support and complement the management team’s ideas and initiatives and also sets out standards of corporate behaviour and ensures compliance with laws and regulations, impacting the Company’s business.

 Board o

 SKF Industria
l is a professionally managed Company

The Company’s Managing Director and Whole Time Director are the Executive Directors on the Board.
 Board ind
ependence is ensured by having

ctors have made necessary disclosures

5.3 Performance evaluation criteria for independent 
dir
ectors
 The Nomination and R
emuneration committee shall 
carry out the evaluation of the Board as a whole, its committee and individual Directors as per the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 and SEBI LODR. The Board shall thereafter review the recommendation of the Nomination and Remuneration Committee. 
 On the basis of the ev
aluation carried out, it shall be 
determined whether to extend the term of appointment of the Independent Directors.
 The Board f
ormally assesses its own performance 
based on parameters which, inter alia, include 
performance of the Board on deciding long-term strategy, rating the composition and mix of Board members, discharging of governance and fiduciary duties, handling critical and dissenting suggestions, etc. The parameters for the performance evaluation of the Directors include contribution made at the Board meeting, attendance, instances of sharing best and next practices, domain knowledge, vision, strategy, engagement with senior management, etc.
 The Chairperson(s) o
f the respective Committees 
shares a report to the Board based on the feedback received from the Committee members on the outcome of the performance evaluation exercise of the Committee.

5.5
 Remuner
ation of Directors:
 1. Remuner
ation to Executive Directors shall involve 
a balance between fixed and incentive pay reflecting the short and long-term performance objectives appropriate to the working of the Company and its goals.
 2. An Independ
ent Director may be paid 
remuneration by way of sitting fees for attending meetings of the Board of Directors or any Committee of the Board of Directors as may be decided by the Board. Such sitting fees shall not be reckoned for the purposes of the percentage of remuneration.
 3. The Independ
ent Directors shall also be entitled 
for reimbursement of any expenses incurred in connection with participation at the meetings of the Board of Directors or any Committee thereof and commission.
 4. An Independ
ent Director shall not be eligible for 
any Stock option Scheme of the Company, if any such scheme exists.
 5. The maximum rem
uneration payable to anyone 
Managing Director or Whole-Time Director or maximum overall remuneration payable to all Directors including Managing Director and Executive Directors will be within overall limits as defined in the Companies Act, 2013.
 6. The Commission pa
yable to the Non-executive 
Directors shall not exceed 1% of the Net Profits of the Company during any financial year.
